[isolated-home]/.agents/skills/repo-audit/references/enforcement.md'","aggregated_output":"# Enforcement\n\nUse this reference when recommending principles and their checks.\nStart from the project's requirements, approved design and observed risks.\nThese examples are candidates, not defaults.\nChecks catch defined mistakes, not every unsound future design decision.\n\n## Contents\n\n- [Record each principle](#record-each-principle)\n- [Example mappings](#example-mappings)\n- [Proof required before adopting an automated rule](#proof-required-before-adopting-an-automated-rule)\n- [Integrate the maintained command](#integrate-the-maintained-command)\n- [Control existing debt](#control-existing-debt)\n- [One authoritative source per rule](#one-authoritative-source-per-rule)\n\n## Record each principle\n\n| Field | Required evidence |\n|---|---|\n| Reason | The project-specific risk or approved requirement, with a useful example |\n| Scope | The packages, modules or public boundaries the rule governs |\n| Source | The existing authoritative document or executable configuration and its exact locator |\n| Enforcement | The concrete constraint, selected check or human review, and expected outcome |\n| Exception policy | The reason, narrow scope and removal condition for an exception, or why none is allowed |\n\nInventory design documents, contribution guidance, scoped agent instructions and native checks before proposing a new rule.\nReuse the formatter, linter, type system or test framework already present when it expresses the required constraint reliably.\nThe language research brief selects any missing native capability for this project.\nUse parsing or dependency analysis for semantic code rules rather than fragile text matching.\nPropose a custom checker only when existing tools cannot express the constraint reliably.\nSeparate formatting from design checks so cosmetic changes cannot conceal an architectural failure.\nAvoid arbitrary file, function or rule-count limits without a project-specific reason.\n\n## Example mappings\n\n| Principle | Concrete rule | Enforcement | Required evidence |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| Keep persistence behind one interface | UI modules cannot import the database adapter | Import boundary analysis resolving project paths and aliases | An allowed public interface import passes and a direct database import fails |\n| Write each rule once | Generated settings match the authoritative source | Deterministic regeneration and comparison | A stale generated value fails |\n| Validate external input | Reject invalid input at the actual public boundary | Behaviour tests and applicable schema or type checks | Invalid input is rejected and valid input succeeds |\n| Keep behaviour stable during cleanup | Existing supported journeys remain compatible | Relevant integration or end-to-end checks | The same supported journeys pass before and after the change |\n| Keep dependencies purposeful | New abstractions serve a current use and a clear responsibility | Human design review against vision and technical design | Review identifies the requirement, responsibility and simpler alternative |\n\nDo not disguise a human design judgement as a deterministic violation.\nGive the reviewer the relevant authority, example and evidence instead.\n\n## Proof required before adopting an automated rule\n\nRequire a representative valid case and a deliberate violation in disposable fixtures.\nThe valid case must pass.\nThe violation must fail with the expected diagnostic and exit code.\nThe clean project must pass the selected final checks.\nUse the selected tool's native rule-testing mechanism where available.\nInclude relevant syntax, aliases, re-exports, generated code and documented exceptions in the proof scope.\nRecord unsupported syntax and dynamic dependencies as coverage limits rather than assuming they pass.\n\nAfter preparing native dependencies in the clean and independently seeded fixtures, run `rule-proof --repo <target> --check-plan <file> --check-id <id> --valid <clean-dir> --violation <seeded-dir> --expect <specific-diagnostic> --json`.\nThe plan follows `schemas/check-plan.json`; the selected check supplies the native child-command object.\nThe command runs separate disposable copies, records versions, exits and diagnostics in scratch, and removes the copies.\nRepeat for each private import, alias or equivalent resolution bypass and forbidden cycle, retaining public imports and permitted dependency direction in the clean control.\nUnavailable native tools, interrupted execution and failed clean setup are blocked, never proof.\n\nCapture the maintained command, tool version, output, exit code and relevant input state directly from execution.\nThe same maintained command must work locally and in the project's CI when the project selects one, without ignoring failures.\nFor behaviour compatibility, preserve agreed acceptance outcomes rather than proving only that unit checks are green.\nThese are evidence requirements for selected enforcement, not a development workflow.\n\n## Integrate the maintained command\n\nUse reviewed selected findings and `apply` to extend the project's existing check command rather than creating a competing entry point.\nThe target repo owns each checker or pins its declared development dependency; running checks must not require an installed bstack skill or agent session.\nScope each package rule in its native configuration or checker, and prove a sibling package's permitted use remains valid.\nApply the complete initial documented baseline through selected debt findings before expecting the audited project to pass.\nKeep every known violation visible with its reason and removal condition; new violations fail and fixed entries must be removed.\nContinue to prove each rule independently on clean fixtures without counting unrelated audited debt as new violations.\n\nFor integration proof, select the maintained command in the check plan, for example executable `npm`, args `[\"run\", \"check\"]`, cwd `.` and versionArgs `[\"--version\"]`.\nRun the same rule-proof interface against separate disposable sources containing the integrated command, installed native dependencies and the accepted baseline, with one new seeded violation in the negative source.\nCapture both exits and the specific new diagnostic; a tool setup failure does not prove enforcement.\nThe valid control must include a permitted sibling-package use where package scope matters.\n\nIf the project selects CI, apply the same maintained command to its configuration as another reviewed edit.\nAn edit that integrates selected checks declares optional `checkIntegration`, a nonempty list of unique command paths, each a nonempty array of string keys.\nFor `package.json`, mark each selected script with a path such as `[[\"scripts\", \"check\"]]`; other changed scripts receive no integration validation.\nFor a selected `.yml` or `.yaml` CI edit, use JSON syntax, which is valid YAML, and mark the exact `run` or `script` paths, such as `[[\"jobs\", \"check\", \"steps\", \"0\", \"run\"]]`.\nThe installed runtime supports only this JSON subset for marked CI edits; it uses Node built-ins and does not parse general YAML.\nEvery selected path must resolve to a string command in the complete proposed bytes; missing commands, deletion and unsupported marked formats fail before writes.\n`continue-on-error` and `allow_failure` on the selected CI command's ancestor objects must be literal `false` when present.\nUnmarked edits, other package scripts, other CI commands and unrelated configuration fields pass through the ordinary protected-write validation unchanged.\nThe plan digest binds these selections alongside the complete proposed bytes.\nSelected commands use literal arguments and fail-fast top-level `&&` chains; line breaks between commands are allowed only after `&&`.\nTokens are nonempty unquoted words or whole double-quoted literals, separated by whitespace.\nOnly ASCII spaces and tabs separate tokens; LF line breaks are allowed after `&&`, and leading/trailing spaces, tabs and LF are ignored.\nCarriage returns are unsupported anywhere in a command, including CRLF, quoted tokens and leading/trailing whitespace.\nUnicode whitespace remains literal; other ASCII control characters are unsupported.\nUnquoted `<` and `>` redirection, including attached forms, and unquoted leading executable `@` prefixes are unsupported in every command of a chain.\nDouble-quoted redirection characters and `@` remain literal, as do unquoted `@` argument characters.\nSingle quotes anywhere, mixed quoted/unquoted tokens and adjacent quoted fragments are unsupported in executable and argument positions.\nBackslashes and shell escaping are unsupported, including inside quoted arguments; use forward-slash paths and quotes for literal spaces.\nPercent and exclamation characters are unsupported in every script token, including double-quoted literals, because Windows can expand environment variables despite quotes.\nDollar signs and backticks are unsupported in every token, including double-quoted literals.\nUnquoted wildcard and bracket patterns, tilde expansion, variable and command substitution, brace expansion, extglobs and caret escaping are unsupported in executable and argument positions.\nQuoted wildcard, bracket and tilde characters remain literal, as do direct child-command argument arrays, which run without a shell.\nEvery decoded word is rejected if it contains `|`, `&`, `;` or a newline, including quoted arguments.\nQuotes do not make shell control syntax acceptable as an argument.\nFailure-masking operators (`||`, pipelines, separators, background execution and negation), unquoted shell comments, assignment prefixes, shell dispatch and substitutions fail with `ignored-check-failure` before any writes.\nShell dispatch includes `command`, `builtin`, shell executables, executable paths to those programs and their `.exe`/`.cmd`/`.bat` forms in every `&&` segment.\n`npm exec`/`npm x` and `npx` reject `-c` and `--call` modes, including attached option values; `env`, including `env -S`, remains unsupported.\nThis bounded grammar is a configuration safeguard, not an interpreter for arbitrary shell programs or a proof of a checker's implementation.\nThe identical maintained command must still pass the valid disposable control and reject the seeded violation with its diagnostic and nonzero exit through `rule-proof`.\nCI examples are unverified adapters until actual hosted execution is observed; local disposable proof does not establish a merge gate.\n\n## Control existing debt\n\nFix selected findings and keep the remaining findings visible.\nWhen immediate cleanup is unsuitable, propose a narrow record of known violations for the user to accept as temporary debt.\nPrefer the native tool's baseline or suppression mechanism where it exists.\nNew violations must fail, and fixed entries must leave the debt record so it shrinks as fixes land.\nDo not silently refresh a baseline to accept new failures.\nAn exception must record its reason, scope and removal condition.\nNever disable a whole rule or exclude a whole maintenance uses the approved project sources and the current diff; it does not repeat the goal interview or the full repo audit.\nResolve the target, authorised change, explicit comparison base, contract path and selected review or delivery process first.\nRead only affected sources and implementation, widening that scope when the evidence exposes a dependency or unresolved decision.\n\n## Contents\n\n- Collect the final scope\n- Assess every candidate\n- Validate locally\n- Validate a standalone checkout\n- Supply the selected delivery gate\n\n## Collect the final scope\n\nUse Node 24 or later and Git.\nRun the installed helper from its actual skill location; examples below use this checkout's location.\n\n```sh\nnode skills/repo-audit/scripts/repo-audit.mjs contract validate affected sources, then recollect after implementation so the assessment covers the final committed, staged, unstaged and new paths, including both sides of renames and deleted paths.\nUse `--base empty` only before the first commit.\nAn unavailable base blocks comparison; fetch the required object rather than substituting a passing empty diff.\nFor a shallow checkout, run `git fetch --unshallow origin`, then `git fetch origin <required-base-commit>`.\nThe default contract is `.bstack/project.json`; pass `--contract <repo-relative-path>` when the project selected another location.\nCustom previous authority needs `--previous-contract <base-policy-path>` on collection and validation.\nKeep previous and proposed coverage when scopes, checks or document registrations change.\nA missing or incompatible previous policy requires reconciliation or a reviewed migration, not silently reduced coverage.\nA first contract requires the explicitly selected foundation finding.\n\nCollection returns a scratch assessment skeleton at `data.path` without editing the project.\nComplete that change-specific record instead of adding an agent activity log to the design or glossary.\nRecompute candidates after source changes, integration or rebasing, and refresh review and affected execution evidence when relevant inputs or the comparison base change.\n\n## Assess every candidate\n\nInspect each candidate document against the intended behaviour and the actual before/after implementation.\nAssess every unmapped path explicitly too; unknown paths cannot bypass impact review.\nUse the installed `schemas/evidence.schema.json` and `schemas/impact-assessment.json` formats.\nEach entry names `changedBehavior`, all relevant `changedPaths`, a concrete `reason`, literal source `citations` with `path`, `pointer` and `version: base|current`, and `dependentWork`.\nA document entry must cite that authoritative document itself.\nList affected authoritative rule IDs in `coverage`.\n\n| Change | Source to inspect | Assessment |\n|---|---|---|\n| Concept, name or meaning | Relevant glossary | Update confirmed language or explain the preserved definition |\n| Responsibility, interface or dependency | Design and accepted decisions | Describe the actual structure and any approved decision change |\n| Review expectation or repeated error | Existing standards source | Clarify the rule or propose a deterministic check |\n| Purpose, boundary or non-goal | Vision | Surface the intent decision before dependent implementation |\n| Setup, commands or document location | Contributor guidance and agent pointers | Keep maintained command and authority links accurate |\n| Internal change preserving a contract | Relevant sources for that area | Explain specifically why the documented behaviour remains true |\n\nChoose `updated`, `no-impact` or `decision-needed` for each entry.\nAn update includes `delta: {\"before\": \"old excerpt\", \"after\": \"new excerpt\"}` naming the actual changed definition or rule; empty excerpts represent additions or deletions.\nA changed date, comment, whitespace or hash alone is not substantive maintenance.\nKeep correct documents unchanged.\n\nA substantive no-impact reason connects the concrete implementation change to the cited invariant that still holds.\nFor example: “The pricing implementation now names its intermediate total; DESIGN.md still requires quantity multiplied by the unit price, and the public quote and checkout outcomes are unchanged.”\nSupport that claim with the actual diff, literal authoritative excerpts and relevant executed checks.\n“Internal change”, “tests pass” or “docs unaffected” alone does not explain impact.\nAn existing failing journey remains failed even if the changed function's unit checks pass.\nIf code violates an approved rule, propose correcting the code rather than changing the rule to excuse it.\n\nUse `decision-needed` with concrete `dependentWork` when intent, accepted architecture, an exception or conflicting authority needs an owner decision beyond the authorised scope.\nKeep dependent work blocked until the decision Never edit the artifact to chase a layout issue the user has not queued. The only exception is a fatal artifact_failures response, which means the review surface itself could not be used. A background poll is allowed only through a harness-native tracked background-job facility whose completion result is guaranteed to resume or notify the same agent. Never use `nohup`, shell `&`, `disown`, redirected fire-and-forget processes, or a detached terminal without an explicit verified callback merely to keep polling alive. notes[2]: \"Compose DaisyUI components with Tailwind utilities for spacing, grid, flex, width, and typography.\",Prefer component classes over custom CSS for common UI.\nplaybook:\n  id: table\n status and severity so the table remains readable when printed or skimmed.\n  pitfalls[3]: Do not paste a terminal table into HTML and call it done.,Do not hide the important conclusion below a large include an action control that queues a specific prompt.\",\"When one action covers multiple rows and completeness matters, also follow the input playbook's tracked batch pattern so the reviewer submits one explicit ID set for item-by-item accounting.\"\nplaybook:\n  id: input\n  use_when: \"Must be used when the agent needs to collect user input on decisions, choices, preferences, triage, scope, or other structured feedback from within the artifact\"\n  choose[4]: \"Use this when the user needs to select, tune, triage, annotate, or edit a structured choice.\",Use controls for decisions the user can make faster visually than by writing a prompt.,\"Use an opt-in tracked batch when the agent must preserve completeness across a multi-item decision, such as findings to fixes, constraints to implementation, or recommendations to follow-up work.\",Use plain annotations when the artifact only needs open-ended feedback.\n  structure[5]: \"Make each decision surface visible: what is being chosen, what the options mean, and what happens next.\",\"For a tracked batch, give every candidate item a short, stable, visible ID and let the reviewer select or disposition items with editable native controls.\",Keep reversible selection state local in the artifact until the user explicitly submits that question.,Pair each question with a Submit or Queue answer control that sends exactly one prompt for the final answer.,Show selected state separately from queued state so the user trusts what will be sent back.\n  design_rules[10]: \"Native controls - radios, checkboxes, text inputs, selects, textareas, buttons, options, labels, disclosure summaries, and contenteditable regions - are interactive automatically: clicks toggle, focus, and type instead of annotating, so they do not need data-lavish-action. Build choice and option UIs from these whenever you can.\",\"For reversible choices, do not call window.lavish.queuePrompt() from radio change handlers or option click handlers. Those handlers should only update local selected state.\",Use a per-question form submit or explicit Queue answer button to read the current values and call window.lavish.queuePrompt() exactly once for the final answer.,Put data-lavish-action only on custom (non-native) elements that should act like a feedback control - typically a styled div or span you made clickable - so Lavish does not annotate it and shows a pointer cursor instead.,Use data-lavish-question on a question wrapper or pass queueKey when multiple pre-send updates should replace the prior unsent answer for the same question.,\"Pass options such as tag, text, selector, target, data, queueKey, or element when they help the agent understand exactly what the user chose.\",\"For a tracked batch, queue the final selected set once in a concise, bounded data.items array; include each item's stable ID, concise label, and requested disposition, and explicitly tell the agent to account for every submitted ID before reporting completion.\",Call window.lavish.sendQueuedPrompts() only when the control should immediately send committed feedback instead of waiting for the user to press Send to Agent.,Make queued prompts specific enough that the agent can act without asking a follow-up question.,Keep native browser controls accessible and readable on mobile.\n  pitfalls[4]: \"Do not queue one prompt per radio change, checkbox toggle, dropdown change, or choice-button click when the user can still change their mind.\",Do not create controls whose queued prompt is unclear or too vague to execute.,Do not hide the difference between selected locally and queued for the agent.,Do not require interaction for content the user only needs to read.\n  lavish_notes[7]: Lavish is strongest when the artifact becomes a focused review surface and not just a static page.,\"A native single-choice question should submit the final value: `<form data-lavish-question=\\\"plan\\\" onsubmit=\\\"event.preventDefault(); Account for every submitted ID before reporting completion.', { tag: 'tracked-batch', text: 'Apply ' + selected.length + ' selected review items', element: event.currentTarget, data: { items: selected } });\\\"><label><input type=\\\"checkbox\\\" name=\\\"items\\\" value=\\\"R-03\\\" data-label=\\\"Preserve rollback behavior\\\" data-disposition=\\\"must-address\\\"> R-03 — Preserve rollback behavior</label><label><input type=\\\"checkbox\\\" name=\\\"items\\\" value=\\\"R-08\\\" data-label=\\\"Reuse the existing error surface\\\" data-disposition=\\\"must-address\\\"> R-08 — Reuse the existing error surface</label><button type=\\\"submit\\\">Queue selected items</button></form>\",\"The agent's tracked-batch completion receipt must give every submitted ID exactly one outcome: addressed with concrete evidence, deferred with a reason, or rejected with a reason. {"type":"item.completed","item":{"id":"item_8","type":"command_execution","command":"[host-home]/.nix-profile/bin/zsh -lc 'cat [isolated-home]/.agents/skills/repo-audit/references/architecture.md'","aggregated_output":"# Architecture\n\nUse this reference when recommending module boundaries.\nStart with the reported area, stated next change, approved design and relevant history.\nExpand only where evidence leads.\nFor a new project, review expected changes against proposed boundaries instead of inventing history.\n\nCoupling means that a change needs knowledge of, or edits to, another part.\nCohesion means that code belongs together for a clear responsibility.\nA module is a logical unit with an interface and implementation, which may span several private files.\nA god file mixes unrelated responsibilities and becomes a shared target for different changes.\nLength alone does not establish that problem.\n\n## Principles to consider\n\nChoose principles only where project evidence supports them.\nNo single architecture style is required.\n\n| Principle | Practical rule | Source |\n|---|---|---|\n| Single responsibility and cohesion | Keep code together when it changes for the same business reason and separate unrelated reasons | [Single responsibility](https://blog.cleancoder.com/uncle-bob/2014/05/08/SingleReponsibilityPrinciple.html) |\n| Information hiding | Hide a changeable design decision behind an interface so callers do not depend on its representation | [Module decomposition](https://www.cs.lafayette.edu/~gexia/cs301/resources/parnas.html) |\n| Locality of change | Keep capability code and tests together where this reduces coordination across unrelated areas | [Vertical slices](https://www.jimmybogard.com/vertical-slice-architecture/) |\n| Explicit dependency direction | Define allowed dependencies and check forbidden imports and cycles across intended boundaries | [Dependency hierarchy](https://www.cs.lafayette.edu/~gexia/cs301/resources/parnas.html) |\n| Separate wiring from behaviour | Startup assembles modules while modules own business behaviour | [Composition roots](https://blog.ploeh.dk/2011/07/28/CompositionRoot/) |\n| Proportional deployment choices | A single deployed application can retain firm module boundaries | [Module boundaries](https://www.martinfowler.com/articles/microservice-trade-offs.html) |\n\nA deep module hides useful complexity behind a small interface.\nConsolidate tightly related shallow modules when removing their layer concentrates complexity in one place.\nSeparate unrelated responsibilities when their changes currently require shared edits.\nAvoid forwarding layers that add navigation without hiding useful complexity.\nOrganise ownership around domain knowledge rather than scattering one rule across load, validate and save phases.\nThese adaptations follow the pinned design and module guidance attributed in the package NOTICE.\n\n## What the audit inspects\n\n- Unrelated business responsibilities, or startup, UI, domain rules and persistence combined without clear internal boundaries.\n- Imports of private implementation, leaked representations and cycles across intended module boundaries.\n- Shared mutable state and multiple writers of canonical data.\n- Frequently changed files and pairs that repeatedly change together.\n- Central registries, broad utilities, shared type collections and wiring files that unrelated work repeatedly edits.\n- Tests requiring unrelated modules or asserting implementation details rather than a public contract.\n- Two representative planned changes, their write paths and the shared contracts they would modify.\n\nCollect dependency edges through the native analysis selected by the language research brief.\nResolve aliases, re-exports and package entry points.\nReport unsupported syntax, dynamic dependencies and unresolved imports as coverage limits.\nUnresolved imports do not demonstrate independence.\nPrefer an existing architecture checker to another dependency engine.\n\nFor history, record revision range, rename handling and exclusions for generated files, lockfiles and broad formatting changes.\nShow the supporting commits behind a finding.\nCo-change is distinct from observed merge conflicts.\nHistory may omit abandoned rebases and conflict resolutions.\n\n## Signals that are not violations\n\nSize, import counts and change frequency indicate where to investigate.\nThey do not prove a bad boundary.\nA large cohesive domain owner may be sound.\nA thin startup file may legitimately import many modules for wiring.\nA stable domain contract may have many callers.\nCo-change may reflect good cohesion, a mechanical commit or harmful coupling.\nInspect the reasons before recommending a split or consolidation.\nDo not infer conflicts or prescribe services from these signals.\n\n## Boundary rules\n\nName each module's responsibility, owned knowledge, public contract and private implementation.\nDefine allowed dependency direction and the scope of forbidden imports and cycles.\nCallers use the public interface and do not depend on private storage or representation.\nPrivate implementation may span files without exposing additional public entry points.\nKeep startup focused on assembly and put behaviour with its domain owner.\nRemove unnecessary shared write targets before serializing work.\nWhere sharing is real, enforce one writer and sequence unavoidable shared edits.\n\nReview two representative changes against proposed boundaries.\nFor example, a pricing-policy change and a notification-template change should remain inside their owners when the public contracts still suffice.\nCheck whether either also changes a shared type, registry or data representation.\nCompare declared paths and contract changes, then review semantic dependencies even when paths are disjoint.\nA script reports overlap evidence but cannot prove independence or predict every conflict.\nFewer unrelated shared edits are an expected benefit, not a measured reduction until actual evidence exists.\n\nRecord each proposed boundary's project-specific reason, source, scope, enforcement and exception policy.\nReuse existing design decisions and standards rather than duplicating their rules.\nPresent evidenced candidates and alternatives for selection before applying changes.\nVerification requires representative allowed and forbidden imports and compatible public behaviour, not file-count targets.\nUse rule-proof for a clean public-import control and independent private, alias or equivalent resolution bypass and cycle violations with the selected native check.\nThe Python equivalent-resolution fixture exercises an intermediate public-looking bridge to private implementation; proof does not establish every possible re-export or dynamic dependency.\nMaintain these coverage limits alongside the rule evidence.\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}
